TITLE: Fishing &amp; Sunset Combo Charter in Marbella 2036: Best of Both

Home › Fishing & Sunset Combo Charter in Marbella 2036: Best of Both Combine the thrill of reeling in Mediterranean fish with the romance of a sunset cruise on a Marbella fishing and sunset combo charter 2036 . This 4-hour experience from Puerto Banús gives you two hours of guided fishing near Cabopino and Cala del Faro, followed by two hours of cruising past the Golden Mile with free-flow cava and snacks. Prices start at €749 for up to 11 guests — rod hire, bait, and catch prep included. How the combo works The trip runs 15:00–19:00, with the first two hours dedicated to fishing. The skipper motors out to known reefs and drop-offs where sea bass, bream, and mackerel are common. After you've had your fill, we turn west towards Marbella's coastline. The final two hours are pure relaxation: soft drinks, beer, cava, and light snacks as the sun sinks behind La Concha mountain. Timing adjusts seasonally so you always catch the best light. Boats available for the combo Two boats in our fleet are pre-configured for this combo at a flat €749: Azimut 39 (12.5m, 11 guests) — spacious flybridge for sunset views Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 guests) — open cockpit ideal for rod work Both depart from Puerto Banús and include a licensed skipper, fuel, insurance, and all taxes. For larger groups (up to 12), the Mangusta 80 is available — pricing on request via WhatsApp. What you'll catch The Mediterranean off Marbella is rich with species. Expect sea bass, gilthead bream, mackerel, and occasional bonito. The skipper knows the best spots near Cabopino and Cala del Faro, and will provide bait, rods, and reels. No fishing licence is needed — it's included in the charter. After the trip, the crew will clean and fillet your catch for you to take back to your accommodation or have cooked at a local restaurant. Sunset cruising: the golden hour From 17:00, we cruise east along the Marbella coastline. You'll pass the Golden Mile, Marbella Marina, and the Sierra Blanca foothills. The skipper will find a calm anchorage near Cala Cortés or Río Verde for the best photo opportunities. Onboard, the bar opens: cava, white wine, beer, soft drinks, and light snacks are all included. The boat has a sound system — feel free to connect your playlist. Inclusions and extras The price covers everything you need for a worry-free afternoon: Licensed English- and Spanish-speaking skipper Fuel and port fees Fishing rods, reels, bait, and terminal tackle Catch cleaning and filleting Water, soft drinks, beer, cava, white wine, and light snacks Insurance and Spanish IVA (21%) You are welcome to bring your own food or drinks. The boat has a cooler and ice. Glass bottles are discouraged during the fishing leg for safety. What to bring Sun cream, sunglasses, a hat, and a light jacket for the evening breeze. Non-slip shoes are recommended for the fishing leg. If you plan to swim (conditions permitting), bring a towel and swimwear. The boat has a fresh-water shower on deck. Booking and cancellation Book via WhatsApp or our online form. We require a 50% deposit to confirm; the balance is due on the day. Free cancellation up to 48 hours before departure. If the Port Authority closes the harbour due to weather, we reschedule or refund in full. The combo is available year-round, with sunset timing adjusted for the season. Frequently asked questions What species can we catch on the fishing leg? Common catches include sea bass, bream, mackerel, and bonito. The skipper knows local hotspots near Cabopino and Cala del Faro. You can keep your catch and the crew will prepare it for you to take back to your accommodation. Do we need a fishing license? No. The charter includes a recreational fishing license for all guests aboard. Just bring sun protection and a sense of adventure — the skipper handles the paperwork. What happens if the weather is bad? Your safety comes first. If the Port Authority closes the harbour, we reschedule at no cost or issue a full refund. We monitor forecasts 48 hours ahead and advise you promptly. Can we bring our own food or drinks? Yes, you are welcome to bring additional snacks or drinks. The charter already includes water, soft drinks, beer, cava, and light snacks. Glass bottles are discouraged for the fishing leg. Is this suitable for children? Absolutely. The boat has safety equipment for all ages. Children under 12 must wear a life jacket on deck. The crew are experienced with families and make the fishing fun for kids. What time does the sunset leg start? Departure is at 15:00 year-round. The first two hours are dedicated to fishing. From 17:00–19:00, we cruise along the coast as the sun sets. In summer, sunset is around 21:30; in winter, around 18:00 — the itinerary adjusts accordingly. Can we choose which boat? You can select the Azimut 39 (11 guests) or Astondoa 40 (9 guests) at €749 for the full 4-hour combo. Both depart from Puerto Banús. For larger groups, we recommend the Mangusta 80 (12 guests) — pricing on request via WhatsApp. What happens to our catch after the trip? The crew will clean, fillet, and pack your fish in ice for you to take home. Many guests arrange with a local restaurant to cook it for dinner. Just ask the skipper for recommendations. Home › Fishing & Sunset Combo Charter in Marbella 2037 Imagine reeling in a feisty amberjack off Cabopino as the sun dips behind La Concha, then sipping cava while cruising back to Puerto Banús under a sherbet sky. That’s the fishing and sunset combo charter Marbella 2037 — two iconic Costa del Sol experiences wrapped into one seamless trip. Departing from Puerto Banús, this 4- to 8-hour charter pairs sportfishing with a sunset cruise, all included: skipper, fuel, drinks, snacks, and fishing tackle on request. Whether you’re a seasoned angler or a first-timer, it’s the best way to spend a day on the water. From the moment you step aboard, the crew handles everything. You’ll head to prime fishing grounds near Cala del Faro or the Río Verde estuary, where bream, sea bass, mackerel, and amberjack are common. After a few hours of casting, the boat turns west to catch the sunset over the Mediterranean. The Golden Mile glows, the mountains turn violet, and you toast the day with a glass of cava. This is Marbella at its finest. Why Choose a Combo Charter? Most boat trips are either fishing or sunset cruising — rarely both. This combo gives you the thrill of the catch and the romance of the sunset in one booking. You save time, money, and logistics. Plus, the timing works perfectly: fish during the late afternoon bite (16:00–19:00), then cruise into the golden hour (20:00–21:30 in summer). The skipper knows exactly where to position the boat for the best light. It’s also flexible. Want to fish longer? Add hours. Prefer a shorter sunset cruise? Book a 4-hour slot. The boat is yours for the duration — no fixed schedule. Our Fleet for the Combo We offer a range of boats from Puerto Banús. For small groups (up to 8 pax), the Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39 are excellent choices at €749/2h. For larger parties (up to 12 pax), the Mangusta 80 comes with a free jet ski and starts at €4,719 for 4 hours. Common catches include: Bream (Dorada) — 1–3 kg, near rocky bottoms Sea Bass (Lubina) — 2–5 kg, around river mouths Mackerel (Caballa) — 0.5–1 kg, in schools Amberjack (Medregal) — 5–15 kg, deeper waters Red Snapper (Pargo) — 2–4 kg, near reefs The skipper will adjust the location based on season and weather. Summer favours mackerel and bream; autumn brings amberjack. All tackle is provided — just bring your sunglasses and sunscreen. Sunset Cruise Route After fishing, the boat cruises west along the coast. You’ll pass: Marbella Marina — luxury yachts and beach clubs Golden Mile — hotels and mansions lit by the setting sun La Concha Mountain — the iconic peak turns pink and orange Puerto Banús — return to the port as lights flicker on The skipper will anchor at a calm spot for photos and drinks. The sunset lasts about 30 minutes, but the afterglow lingers for another 20. Perfect for that Instagram shot. What’s Included Every charter includes: Licensed skipper Fuel Water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava Light snacks (olives, crisps, nuts) Insurance and safety equipment Spanish IVA (21%) Fishing tackle on request No hidden fees. You can bring your own food or order catering (ask us). The crew speaks English and Spanish. How to Book Ready to combine fishing and sunset? Contact us via WhatsApp or use the booking form on our site . Tell us your preferred date, group size, and boat. We’ll check availability and send a secure payment link. Advance booking is recommended — sunset slots are popular, especially in July and August. For groups larger than 12, we can arrange multiple boats. Just ask. Yes, we provide rod, reel, bait, and tackle on request at no extra cost. Just let us know when you book. The skipper knows the best fishing spots around Cabopino and Cala del Faro. What time does the sunset part start? The sunset cruise typically runs from 20:00 to 21:30 in summer (May–September) and 18:00 to 19:30 in winter (October–April). Your skipper will adjust the itinerary so you catch the golden hour over the Mediterranean. Can I keep the fish I catch? Yes, you can keep your catch. The crew can clean and bag it for you. Many guests take it to a local restaurant in Puerto Banús or Marbella to have it cooked. Check local size and bag limits – the skipper will advise. What is included in the price? Home › Fishing & Sunset Combo Charter in Marbella 2038 Combine the thrill of sport fishing with the romance of a Mediterranean sunset on a single charter from Marbella. The fishing and sunset combo charter Marbella 2038 lets you spend the afternoon casting lines off the Costa del Sol, then cruise back to Puerto Banús as the sky turns gold and pink. It’s a full-day escape that works for anglers, couples, and small groups alike. How the combo works You meet your skipper at Puerto Banús around 14:00. After a safety briefing, you head west or east depending on the day’s fishing conditions. The skipper knows the best spots near Cabopino, Cala del Faro, or Estepona. You fish for 2–3 hours with provided tackle and bait. Then, as the sun lowers, you motor to a calm anchorage, drop anchor, and enjoy cold drinks, snacks, and the sunset. You’re back at Puerto Banús by 20:00–21:00. Boats for the combo We recommend two boats for this experience: Astondoa 40 – 12.5m, 9 passengers, €749/2h or €2,299/8h Azimut 39 – 12.5m, 11 passengers, same pricing Both depart from Puerto Banús and include a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, and 21% VAT. Fishing gear and bait are included at no extra cost. What’s included in the price Licensed English-speaking skipper Fuel for the entire trip Water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava Light snacks (chips, olives, nuts, fruit) Fishing rods, reels, and bait Insurance and Spanish VAT (21%) Towel set and sun protection on request Best time for the combo The prime season runs from April to October . In June and July, sunset is around 21:30, giving you a long golden hour. Water temperatures in summer reach 24–26 °C, so fish are active. Spring and autumn offer cooler air (20–25 °C) and fewer crowds. Winter charters are possible but sunset is earlier (18:00) and fish are less active. What to bring Sunscreen (SPF 50+) Sunglasses and a hat Light jacket or windbreaker for the evening Camera or phone for sunset photos Swimwear if you want to dip in after fishing Extra snacks or drinks (no glass) Why choose this combo over separate trips Booking a single combo saves time and money. You avoid two separate bookings, two deposits, and two trips to the marina. The flow from fishing to sunset is seamless — no rushing back to shore. And you get the same skipper all day, so the experience feels personal. Prices start at €749 for 2 hours (fishing + sunset) or €2,299 for 8 hours (full day). Nearby anchorages for sunset Depending on wind and swell, your skipper may anchor at: Cala del Faro – a small cove west of Marbella with calm water Cala Cortés – a sheltered bay near Estepona Off Puerto Banús – with direct views of the port lights and La Concha Each spot offers a different perspective of the coast. The skipper will choose the best option for the day. Frequently asked questions What boats are available for the fishing and sunset combo charter? We recommend the Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) or Azimut 39 (12.5m, 11 pax). Both depart from Puerto Banús and include a licensed skipper, fuel, drinks, snacks, and VAT. The price is €749 for 2 hours or €2,299 for 8 hours. Do I need a fishing license? No. Our licensed skipper handles all permits and regulations. You just show up, fish, and enjoy the sunset. Gear and bait are provided. What fish can we catch? Common catches include sea bass, bream, mackerel, and smaller tuna. The skipper will position the boat over productive spots near Cabopino or Estepona. Catch-and-release is encouraged. What if the weather is bad? We monitor conditions closely. If the forecast shows high winds or rough seas, we’ll reschedule at no cost or offer a full refund. Your safety is paramount. Can we bring our own food or drinks? Yes, you’re welcome to bring extra snacks or drinks. The charter already includes water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, and light snacks. No glass bottles, please. How long does the combo charter last? Typically 4–6 hours: 2–3 hours fishing followed by a sunset cruise. For the full experience, book the 8-hour option (€2,299) which allows more fishing time and a relaxed sunset. Is the sunset guaranteed? We can’t control the clouds, but Marbella enjoys over 300 sunny days a year. The sunset views from the sea, with La Concha mountain in the background, are spectacular even with some haze. How do I book? Contact us via WhatsApp (button on our site) or use the booking form. We’ll confirm availability, take a deposit, and send you the meeting point at Puerto Banús. TITLE: Full Day Bottom Fishing Charter in Marbella: What You’ll Catch &amp; Costs
DESCRIPTION: Full day bottom fishing charter Marbella: catch sea bass, bream &amp; grouper from €2,299. Licensed skipper, gear &amp; drinks included. Book now.

Home › Full Day Bottom Fishing Charter in Marbella: What You’ll Catch & Costs If you want to spend a full day on the Mediterranean targeting sea bass, grouper, and bream, a full day bottom fishing charter Marbella is the most direct way to do it. Departing from Puerto Banús, you'll head offshore to rocky reefs and sandy flats where bottom-dwelling fish feed. All gear, bait, drinks, and a licensed skipper are included, so you just show up and fish. Prices start at €2,299 for 8 hours on boats like the Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39, with larger vessels available for groups up to 12. Why Choose a Full Day Bottom Fishing Charter in Marbella Bottom fishing is one of the most reliable techniques in the Mediterranean. You drop a weighted rig with bait to the seabed and wait for species that live near the bottom. Unlike trolling or lure fishing, bottom fishing works in calm or choppy conditions and doesn't require high-speed boat handling. It's ideal for families, groups, or anglers who want a relaxed day on the water with a good chance of catching dinner. The waters off Marbella, from Cabopino to Estepona, hold rocky outcrops and sandy patches that attract sea bass, bream, and grouper year-round. What You’ll Catch: Species and Seasons The Mediterranean seabed around Marbella is home to several prized table fish. Your catch will depend on the month, but here's what to expect: European sea bass (up to 10 kg) – best from May to October. Common bream – abundant all year, especially near rocky bottoms. Red snapper – found in deeper water (20–50 m), common in summer. Grouper – larger specimens near reefs; catch-and-release encouraged. Mackerel – often caught as bycatch; great for bait or eating fresh. Dentex and amberjack – occasional summer visitors for experienced anglers. Water temperatures in June–October range from 18°C to 24°C, which brings more species closer to shore. In winter, sea bass and bream remain active, but you'll need to fish deeper (30–50 m). Full Day Bottom Fishing Charter Costs Our fleet offers two main options for a full day bottom fishing charter in Marbella, with prices that include everything: Boat Length Max Guests Full Day (8h) Price Includes Astondoa 40 12.5 m 9 €2,299 Skipper, fuel, drinks, snacks, gear, VAT Azimut 39 12.5 m 11 €2,299 Skipper, fuel, drinks, snacks, gear, VAT Mangusta 80 24 m 12 From €4,719 (4h min) Includes jet ski; extend to full day on request For other boats like the Ferretti 94 or Canados 86, pricing is available on request via WhatsApp. All prices include 21% Spanish VAT, licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, and safety equipment. Fishing gear (rods, reels, bait) is provided at no extra cost. What to Expect on the Day Your charter departs from Puerto Banús, usually between 8:00 and 9:00 AM. The skipper will take you 3–8 nautical miles offshore to spots marked on GPS from years of experience. You'll anchor or drift over rocky reefs or sandy bottoms. The skipper sets up the rods with weighted rigs and bait (squid, sardines, or worms). You just drop the line and wait for a bite. The boat is equipped with a fish finder to locate schools, so you're not fishing blind. Lunch is served on board: light snacks, drinks, and you can even grill your catch if you keep it. The trip ends around 5:00 PM back at Puerto Banús. Tips for a Successful Full Day Bottom Fishing Charter Book your full day bottom fishing charter Marbella from June to October for the best variety. Bring sunscreen, a hat, and a light jacket – sea breezes can cool things down. Arrive 15 minutes early at Puerto Banús for a briefing on safety and gear. If you want to keep fish, bring a cooler with ice; the skipper can clean your catch. Let the skipper know your experience level – they'll adjust the rigs and depth accordingly. Catch-and-Release and Sustainability We encourage catch-and-release for undersized or protected species, such as grouper under 5 kg or any fish that looks rare. The Mediterranean ecosystem is fragile, and responsible fishing helps keep stocks healthy. The skipper will show you how to handle fish with wet hands to protect their slime coat and release them quickly. Legal-sized sea bass, bream, and mackerel can be kept for dinner – a fresh fish grilled on the boat is hard to beat. Frequently Asked Questions What is a full day bottom fishing charter in Marbella? A full day bottom fishing charter is an 8-hour trip where you fish on the seabed for species like sea bass, grouper, and bream. Departing from Puerto Banús, you'll head 3–8 NM offshore to reefs and sandy bottoms. The boat anchors or drifts, and you use weighted rigs with bait to tempt bottom-dwelling fish. All gear, bait, and a licensed skipper are included. What fish can I catch on a bottom fishing charter in Marbella? Common catches include European sea bass (up to 10 kg), common bream, red snapper, grouper, and mackerel. In summer, you may also hook dentex or amberjack. The Mediterranean seabed around Marbella holds rocky reefs and sandy flats where these species feed. Catches vary with season and tide, but bottom fishing consistently yields good results, especially from June to October. How much does a full day bottom fishing charter cost in Marbella? Prices start at €2,299 for 8 hours on the Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) or Azimut 39 (12.5m, 11 pax). The Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax) costs from €4,719 for a minimum 4 hours, which can be extended to a full day, and includes a free jet ski. All prices include skipper, fuel, drinks, snacks, and 21% VAT. For other boats, pricing is available on request via WhatsApp. What is included in the full day bottom fishing charter? Every charter includes a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, safety equipment, and Spanish IVA (21%). Fishing gear — rods, reels, line, hooks, sinkers, and bait — is provided at no extra cost. You just bring sunscreen, a hat, and a cooler for any fish you keep. Do I need a fishing license for a full day charter? No, you do not need a personal fishing license. The charter boat operates under a commercial fishing permit held by the skipper. You can fish legally as a guest on board. However, any fish you keep must comply with local size and bag limits, which the skipper will explain. Can I keep the fish I catch? Yes, you can keep legal-sized fish for your own consumption. Common species like sea bass and bream have minimum size limits (e.g., sea bass: 36 cm). The skipper will help you measure and handle the catch. We encourage catch-and-release for smaller or protected species to sustain local stocks. What is the best time of year for bottom fishing in Marbella? The best months are June through October when water temperatures range from 18°C to 24°C. This warm period brings species like grouper and red snapper closer to shore. Spring and autumn also offer good fishing, but winter can be windier. Early morning departures (8:00–9:00) often yield the best catches before the sea breeze picks up. How do I book a full day bottom fishing charter in Marbella? TITLE: Marbella Fishing Charter for Bottom Fishing: Species &amp; Techniques
DESCRIPTION: Book a Marbella fishing charter for bottom fishing. Target bream, grouper &amp; snapper with expert skippers from Puerto Banús. Prices from €749 for 2h.

Home › Marbella Fishing Charter for Bottom Fishing: Species & Techniques Bottom fishing from Marbella is one of the most accessible and rewarding ways to fish the Mediterranean. Dropping a baited rig to the seabed off Puerto Banús can yield gilthead bream, grouper, red snapper, and European sea bass. With a Marbella boat charter , you get a licensed skipper who knows the best grounds, all gear included, and a day on the water that combines fishing with the Costa del Sol coastline. Prices start at €749 for 2 hours on a Tier A boat like the Astondoa 40. How Bottom Fishing Works on a Marbella Fishing Charter Bottom fishing, also called ledgering or bait fishing, relies on weight to sink a baited hook to the seabed. The rig sits on the bottom or just above it, targeting fish that feed on crustaceans, molluscs, and smaller fish. Your skipper will anchor over sandy patches near rocky reefs, wrecks, or artificial reefs at depths of 10–40 metres. Bait is typically squid, pilchard, or mackerel strips. The technique is simple: cast, wait for a bite, and strike. It's ideal for beginners and experienced anglers alike. Target Species for Bottom Fishing off Marbella The Mediterranean offers a variety of bottom-dwelling species. Here’s what you can expect: Gilthead Bream — Abundant in spring, found over seagrass beds and sandy bottoms. Fight hard for their size. Grouper — Larger specimens lurk around rocky reefs in summer. Requires heavier gear and patience. Red Snapper — Autumn favourite on deeper sandy grounds. Delicate white flesh. European Sea Bass — Year-round, but best in autumn. Often caught near structures. Common Dentex — Powerful predator, caught on rocky bottoms in summer and winter. Your skipper will adjust bait and rig based on the target species. All catches are subject to Spanish recreational fishing limits. Best Boats for a Bottom Fishing Charter in Marbella Our fleet departs from Puerto Banús and every boat is suitable for bottom fishing. Here are the top picks: Boat Length Capacity Price Best For Astondoa 40 12.5m 9 pax €749/2h – €2,299/8h Small groups, half-day trips Azimut 39 12.5m 11 pax €749/2h – €2,299/8h Friends or family Mangusta 80 (Grey) 24m 12 pax €4,719/4h Luxury + jet ski included Pershing 46 14m 10 pax On request Speed and style All charters include skipper, fuel, drinks, snacks, insurance, and 21% VAT. For a full list, see our Marbella boat rental fleet . When to Book a Marbella Fishing Charter for Bottom Fishing Seasonality matters. Spring (March–May) offers the best bream fishing in shallow waters near Cabopino and Cala del Faro. Summer (June–August) targets grouper and dentex on rocky reefs off Estepona. Autumn (September–November) is prime for red snapper and sea bass on deeper sandy bottoms near Sotogrande. Morning trips (8am–12pm) typically produce more bites. Water temperatures range from 14°C in winter to 26°C in summer, affecting fish activity. What’s Included in Your Bottom Fishing Charter Every booking includes: Licensed, English-speaking skipper All fishing rods, reels, rigs, bait, and terminal tackle Fuel for the full trip Water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava Light snacks (chips, olives, nuts) Insurance and safety equipment Spanish IVA (21%) You only need to bring sun cream, a hat, and a camera. The skipper will clean your catch if you plan to have it cooked at a local restaurant. Techniques and Tips for Bottom Fishing in Marbella Your skipper will use ledger rigs (running or fixed) with a 2–6oz weight depending on depth and current. Drop-shotting can be effective for sea bass over rocky ground. Bait presentation matters: fresh squid outlasts pilchard. If bites are slow, ask the skipper to move to a different mark. The waters off Puerto Banús have several artificial reefs and wrecks that hold fish. For a more active session, combine bottom fishing with trolling for surface predators. Frequently asked questions What is bottom fishing on a Marbella fishing charter? Bottom fishing involves dropping baited rigs to the seabed to target fish that feed near the bottom. On a Marbella fishing charter, you’ll fish over sandy, rocky, or wreck-strewn grounds at depths of 10–40 metres. Common catches include gilthead bream, grouper, red snapper, and European sea bass. Your skipper will handle anchor positioning and bait selection. Which boats are best for bottom fishing? Any boat from our fleet can be used for bottom fishing. The Astondoa 40 (€749/2h) and Azimut 39 are popular for shorter trips. For longer days, the Mangusta 80 (€4,719/4h) offers extra space and a jet ski. All boats depart from Puerto Banús and include a licensed skipper who knows the best bottom-fishing spots. What species can I catch bottom fishing in Marbella? Typical catches include gilthead bream, common dentex, grouper, red snapper, European sea bass, and occasional stingrays or dogfish. Spring brings bream inshore, summer sees grouper on rocky reefs, and autumn offers snapper on deeper sandy bottoms. Your skipper will adapt techniques based on season and conditions. Do I need a fishing license for a bottom fishing charter? No, you do not need a personal fishing license. The charter includes a licensed skipper and all permits for recreational fishing. However, you must follow Spanish size and bag limits. The skipper will advise on catch-and-release practices and which fish can be kept for cooking. What equipment is provided for bottom fishing? All rods, reels, rigs, bait, and terminal tackle are provided. We use medium-heavy rods with 20–30lb line, ledger or paternoster rigs, and baits like squid, pilchard, or mackerel. You only need to bring sun protection, a hat, and a camera. The skipper will rig everything for the target species. Can I keep the fish I catch and have it cooked? Yes, many local restaurants in Puerto Banús and Marbella will cook your catch for a small fee. The skipper can recommend spots. However, we encourage responsible catch-and-release for protected species. Check with your skipper about current regulations before keeping fish. What is the best time of year for bottom fishing in Marbella? Spring (March–May) offers excellent bream fishing in shallower waters. Summer (June–August) targets grouper and dentex on rocky reefs. Autumn (September–November) is prime for red snapper and sea bass on deeper sandy bottoms. Winter fishing is slower but can yield large dentex. Morning trips often produce more bites. Home › Marbella Photoshoot Yacht: Capture Stunning Sunset Moments Sunset over the Mediterranean is a photographer’s dream—warm light, golden reflections, and the silhouette of the Marbella coastline. A Marbella photoshoot yacht sunset session turns that dream into a reality, whether you’re a professional model, an influencer, or celebrating a special occasion. Our fleet departs from Puerto Banús, offering private charters with all essentials included. Prices start at €749 for 2 hours on the Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39, and we can customise the experience for larger groups on yachts like the Mangusta 80. Capture the moment with no hidden fees, just stunning views and a licensed skipper who knows the best spots. Why Choose a Yacht for Your Sunset Photoshoot in Marbella? A yacht provides a mobile platform that changes with the light. As the sun sinks behind La Concha mountain, you can anchor off Cala del Faro or cruise along the Golden Mile. The reflection off the water creates a natural fill light, reducing harsh shadows. Unlike a studio, you get 360-degree views and the ability to move with the sun. TITLE: Marbella Sunrise Fishing Charter: Catch the Dawn Catch
DESCRIPTION: Book a sunrise fishing charter Marbella and reel in the dawn catch from Puerto Banús. Licensed skipper, gear, drinks &amp; snacks included. 5-12 pax.

Home › Marbella Sunrise Fishing Charter: Catch the Dawn Catch There's something primal about casting a line as the sun lifts over La Concha. A sunrise fishing charter Marbella gets you on the water before the jet skis and day-trippers, when the sea is glassy and the fish are hungry. Departing from Puerto Banús, our 4-hour trip combines the thrill of the catch with the quiet beauty of dawn on the Costa del Sol. Licensed skipper, all gear, drinks, and snacks included – just bring your hat. Why book a sunrise fishing charter in Marbella? Fishing at first light isn't just about avoiding the heat (though that helps). Fish feed more actively in low light, and the lack of boat traffic means your bait gets noticed. Our skippers know the productive grounds off Cabopino, Cala del Faro, and the Río Verde mouth. Expect sea bass, bream, mackerel, and the occasional dentex. The 4-hour window – typically 06:30 to 10:30 – gives you prime feeding time and still leaves the rest of the day free for the beach. Our boats for the dawn catch We run the sunrise charter on our Tier A boats: the Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) and Azimut 39 (12.5m, 11 pax). Both are well-equipped with rod holders, a shaded cockpit, and a cooler for your catch. If you have a larger group (up to 12 pax), ask about the Mangusta 80 or Azimut 58 Flybridge – pricing on request via WhatsApp. All charters include a licensed skipper who handles the navigation and knows the local marks. What's included in the price The €749/4h rate (Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39) covers everything: licensed skipper, fuel, all fishing gear and bait, insurance, and Spanish IVA (21%). Onboard you'll find water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, and light snacks. No hidden extras – the price is the price. For the Mangusta 80, the minimum spend is €4,719 for 4 hours (includes a jet ski). Where we fish: best spots near Marbella Our skippers rotate between proven locations depending on wind and tide. Common grounds include: Off Cabopino – rocky bottoms holding bream and sea bass Cala del Faro – deeper water for dentex and mackerel Río Verde mouth – freshwater runoff attracts baitfish and predators Estepona reefs – a longer run but worth it for bigger specimens All within 30 minutes of Puerto Banús. Your skipper decides on the day based on conditions. What to bring and wear Dawn on the Mediterranean can be cool (15–18°C in spring/autumn), so a light windbreaker or fleece is smart. Sunscreen is essential even at 7am – the UV bounces off the water. Polarised sunglasses help you see into the water. No need for specialised gear; we supply everything. Leave the chunky heels at home – non-slip deck shoes or trainers are best. Catch-and-release vs. keeping your catch We encourage catch-and-release to preserve stocks, especially for larger bream and dentex. If you want to keep a few for the barbecue, your skipper will clean and fillet them. Many guests take their catch to a nearby chiringuito (beach bar) – try Los Sardinales in Cabopino – and they'll grill it for you. Please respect local size limits: sea bass under 36cm go back. How to book your sunrise fishing charter Reserve via our Boat Rental Marbella booking page or message us on WhatsApp. We need at least 48 hours' notice for the dawn trip to arrange bait and skipper. What fish can we catch on a Marbella sunrise charter? Typical catches include sea bass, bream, mackerel, and dentex. The Costa del Sol's waters are rich year-round. Your skipper knows the best spots off Cabopino and Cala del Faro. Catch-and-release is recommended, but you can keep a few for the grill. Is fishing gear included in the charter price? Yes, all rods, reels, bait, and tackle are provided. We supply spinning and bottom-fishing setups suitable for the local species. Just bring sunscreen, sunglasses, and a light jacket – it can be cool on the water at dawn. Can we eat the fish we catch? Absolutely. Your skipper can clean and fillet your catch. Many guests take it to a beach club or restaurant that will cook it for you. Otherwise, you're welcome to keep it in a cooler bag for later. What happens if the weather is bad? Your safety comes first. If the forecast shows strong winds or rough seas, we'll reschedule your charter at no extra cost or offer a full refund. No – your skipper holds the necessary permits for recreational fishing in Spanish waters. You just show up and fish. Home › Marbella Sunset Cruise with Champagne & Tapas 2040: Evening Elegance There is no better way to end a Marbella day than aboard a private motor yacht, cruising the Costa del Sol as the sun sinks into the Mediterranean. Our Marbella sunset cruise with champagne & tapas 2040 is a two-hour evening charter from Puerto Banús , designed for couples, small groups, and anyone craving a touch of elegance. With sparkling cava , light tapas, and the silhouette of La Concha mountain behind you, this is the quintessential Marbella sunset experience. What to expect on your sunset cruise Your evening begins at Puerto Banús , where you'll board one of our premium Tier A motor yachts — the Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 guests) or the Azimut 39 (12.5m, 11 guests). Both boats offer a spacious flybridge, comfortable seating, and a shaded saloon. As you cast off, your licensed skipper will guide you along the Marbella coastline, past the Golden Mile and the Marbella Marina , towards the open sea. The onboard cooler is stocked with chilled cava , white wine, beer, soft drinks, and water, plus a selection of light tapas snacks — olives, almonds, local cheeses, and cured meats. The 2-hour duration gives you ample time to relax, take photos, and simply soak in the 2040 sunset colours. Pricing and booking details This experience is available on our Tier A boats at a fixed rate: €749 for 2 hours (€2,299 for a full 8-hour day charter). The price includes the licensed skipper, fuel, all drinks and snacks, insurance, safety equipment, and Spanish IVA (21%). There are no hidden fees. For larger groups or custom requests, contact us via WhatsApp for a personalised quote. Bookings require a 50% deposit; the balance is due 7 days before departure. Cancellations made 14+ days in advance receive a full refund. The perfect evening itinerary Your sunset cruise typically departs 1 hour before sunset — between 19:00 and 20:00 depending on the season. The route heads west along the coast, offering uninterrupted views of the Marbella skyline , the La Concha mountain, and the distant Estepona hills. As the sun dips below the horizon, the sky turns shades of orange, pink, and purple — a spectacle best enjoyed with a glass of cava in hand. The skipper will anchor at a calm spot near Cala del Faro or Cala Cortés for the final moments of daylight. You'll return to Puerto Banús after dusk, with the marina lights reflecting on the water. Why choose a private sunset cruise over a group tour? A private charter offers exclusivity, flexibility, and personalised service. Unlike crowded group sunset tours, you control the music, the pace, and the company. The Astondoa 40 and Azimut 39 are well-maintained, comfortable yachts with plenty of space for socialising or quiet moments. The included cava and tapas elevate the experience without the need for extra planning. Whether it's a romantic evening, a birthday celebration, or a corporate reward, a private sunset cruise ensures memories that last long after the sun sets. What to bring and wear Evening temperatures on the water can be cooler than on land, especially after sunset. Bring a light jacket or sweater. Comfortable shoes with non-slip soles are recommended. Don't forget your camera or phone for photos — the sunset views are spectacular. Sunscreen is still advisable even in the late afternoon. All drinks and snacks are provided, but you are welcome to bring additional food or drinks if you wish. Life jackets are available for all guests, including children. Other sunset charter options If your group is larger or you prefer a different style, we offer sunset cruises on other boats in our fleet. The Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m, 12 guests) and the Pershing 46 (14m, 10 guests) are also available — pricing on request. TITLE: Marbella Sunset Snorkeling Combo Cruise from Puerto Banús
DESCRIPTION: Book a Marbella sunset snorkeling combo cruise from Puerto Banús. Swim in Cala del Faro, sip cava at sea, and watch the sun dip behind La Concha. From €749.

Home › Marbella Sunset Snorkeling Combo Cruise from Puerto Banús A Marbella sunset snorkeling combo cruise from Puerto Banús is the most efficient way to fit two of the Costa del Sol’s best experiences into a single afternoon. You get a sheltered cove for swimming and snorkelling while the sun is still high, then a leisurely cruise back past the Golden Mile as the sky turns orange and pink. No rushing, no crowds – just your group, a skipper, and an open bar. How the combo works The charter runs for four hours, split roughly 50/50. You depart Puerto Banús at around 16:00 (or 17:00 in high summer) and head east or west to one of two coves: Cala del Faro (near the Marbella lighthouse) or Cala Cortés (just past Cabopino). The skipper drops anchor in 3–6 m of clear water, hands out masks and fins, and you have about 90 minutes to swim, snorkel, or simply float on a paddleboard if you prefer. At around 18:00–18:30 you weigh anchor and motor back along the coast. The return leg takes you past the Marbella Marina, the Golden Mile, and finally into Puerto Banús as the sun sets behind La Concha mountain. Drinks remain open throughout – cava for a toast, beer, wine, soft drinks, and water. Which boats are used This experience runs on two boats from our fleet: Astondoa 40 – 12.5 m, max 9 guests, with a spacious sundeck and a small cabin for changing. Azimut 39 – 12.5 m, max 11 guests, with a larger cockpit and a wet bar. Both are Tier A boats priced at €749 for 4 hours or €2,299 for 8 hours if you want a full day. The price includes the skipper, fuel, insurance, Spanish IVA, snorkel gear, and the open bar with snacks. You can see the full fleet and compare options on our boat rental Marbella page. Snorkelling conditions and marine life The Costa del Sol is not a coral reef destination, but the rocky bottoms around Cala del Faro and Cala Cortés host a surprising variety of fish. Expect to see sea bream, damselfish, wrasse, and the occasional octopus hiding in crevices. Water visibility ranges from 5–15 m depending on wind and recent swell. Late afternoon light actually improves visibility because the sun is lower and glare is reduced. Water temperature in summer (June–September) is 22–26°C – a shorty wetsuit is optional. In May and October it drops to 18–21°C, so we recommend a thin 3 mm wetsuit (we can provide one on request). The coves are sheltered from the prevailing easterly wind, so the surface is usually calm. Sunset timing and best months Sunset in Marbella varies by season: Month Sunset time Departure time May 21:15 17:00 June 21:40 17:00 July 21:40 16:00 August 21:15 16:00 September 20:30 16:00 October 19:45 15:00 The best months for this combo are June through September , when the sea is warmest and the sunset lasts longer. May and October are also good but cooler – bring a light jacket for the return cruise. What to bring and what to expect Swimwear, towel, sunscreen (reef-safe preferred), and a light jumper for the evening breeze. Your own snorkel mask if you have a prescription – we provide standard ones. A camera or phone – the sunset light on La Concha is worth a few shots. No red wine – it stains the upholstery. White wine, cava, and beer are provided. The skipper handles all navigation and anchoring. You do not need a boat licence – this is a private charter with a licensed professional. The boat has a marine toilet and a fresh-water shower on the swim platform for rinsing off after snorkelling. How to book This experience is available on request – we do not have a live calendar. To reserve, send us a WhatsApp message with your preferred date, group size, and whether you want the Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39. We will confirm availability within 2 hours. Payment is by bank transfer or card link. Cancellations are free up to 48 hours before departure. For a full list of boats and other experiences, visit our boat rental Marbella page. Frequently asked questions What time does the sunset snorkeling combo start? Typically 16:00 or 17:00 depending on the season. The first two hours are spent at a sheltered cove for snorkelling, then you cruise back towards Puerto Banús as the sun sets around 20:30–21:30. Your skipper will confirm the exact departure time after booking. Which coves do we snorkel at? We use Cala del Faro or Cala Cortés, both within 20–30 minutes of Puerto Banús. Cala del Faro has a rocky seabed with sea bream and occasional octopus. Cala Cortés is slightly sandier and more sheltered. The skipper chooses based on wind and sea conditions. Is snorkelling equipment provided? Yes – masks, snorkels, and fins are included at no extra cost. We carry adult sizes and a few youth options. If you have your own prescription mask, bring it along. Life jackets are also available for non-swimmers. What is included in the €749 price? The price covers the full 4-hour charter, a licensed skipper, fuel, insurance, Spanish VAT (21%), snorkel gear, and an open bar of water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, and cava. Light snacks (olives, crisps, nuts) are also provided. Can we bring our own food and drink? Yes – you are welcome to bring extra snacks, a picnic, or your own beverages. The boat has a small fridge and a cooler box. Glass bottles are fine, but please avoid red wine (stains). We recommend a light meal before boarding. What happens if the weather is bad? Your safety is our priority. If the skipper deems conditions unsafe (high wind, thunderstorms, or rough seas), we will reschedule at no cost or issue a full refund. We monitor forecasts 48 hours ahead and will notify you. Can we stay out longer than 4 hours? Yes – the Astondoa 40 and Azimut 39 can be booked for 8 hours (€2,299) if you want a full day at sea. That gives you 3–4 hours of snorkelling plus a longer sunset cruise. Contact us via WhatsApp to arrange. Do we need to be experienced swimmers? No – the skipper will anchor in calm, shallow water (3–6 m deep) so you can stand or float easily. Life jackets are mandatory for non-swimmers. We also carry a floating ring. TITLE: Multi-Day Yacht Charter Marbella: 3-Day Costa del Sol Itinerary
DESCRIPTION: Plan your multi day yacht charter Marbella with our detailed 3-day Costa del Sol itinerary, 12-pax yachts, skipper included. WhatsApp us today to book.

Home › Multi-Day Yacht Charter Marbella: 3-Day Costa del Sol Itinerary A multi day yacht charter Marbella turns the Costa del Sol into a proper cruising ground rather than a single afternoon swim stop. Over 72 hours you can run 60–90 nautical miles westward, anchor in three or four genuinely different coves, sleep in two marinas, and still have La Concha mountain in your wake by Tuesday morning. This guide walks through a tested 3-day itinerary aboard our 24m+ flagships — the Mangusta 80 , Canados 86 or Ferretti 94 — with licensed skipper, fuel and drinks already in the price. Browse the full fleet first if you have not picked a yacht. Why 3 days beats a day charter A single 4-hour day charter from Puerto Banús typically covers the Golden Mile, Marbella Marina and a swim at Río Verde — beautiful, but you never leave the bay. A 3-day charter doubles your cruising radius. You reach Sotogrande, which is 22 NM west and impossible to enjoy on a half-day run, anchor under the cliffs of Cala del Faro near Estepona, and watch the Strait of Gibraltar traffic in the distance. You also get two evenings on the water: dinner on the flybridge, swimming at sunset, breakfast at anchor. The maths are simple — at Tier B €4,719 for 4 hours, three full days of cruising delivers far more value per cruising hour than three separate day trips. Day 1 — Puerto Banús, Marbella, Cabopino Boarding is at 10:00 in Puerto Banús. The first leg is a 12 NM eastbound run past the Golden Mile, the lighthouse and Marbella Marina, ending at Cabopino — the dune-backed beach 20 minutes east of town. Lunch aboard, swim, paddleboard, then push a further 6 NM east towards La Cala de Mijas if the seas are calm. By 17:00 the skipper re-crosses to Puerto Banús for the first overnight: many guests sleep aboard at the berth, others book a hotel five minutes from the marina. Dinner is your choice — Puerto Banús has 30+ restaurants within a short walk and the skipper can recommend the quieter rooftop spots away from the strip if you do not fancy the front-row scene. Day 2 — Westbound to Estepona and Sotogrande Day two is the long cruising day. We leave Puerto Banús at 09:30, run 14 NM west past the Golden Mile to Estepona for a mid-morning coffee stop and stretch ashore. From Estepona it is another 11 NM down to Sotogrande, with a 90-minute anchor stop at Cala del Faro — protected, deep blue, ideal for swimming and the SeaBob if your yacht carries one. Arrival in Sotogrande marina by 18:00. Sotogrande is the quietest of the three marinas, and the polo and beach-club scene makes for a different evening from Puerto Banús. Sleep aboard or in one of the marina hotels — the skipper helps arrange the berth and a concierge for restaurant bookings. Day 3 — Sotogrande back to Puerto Banús via Río Verde The return leg is shorter and slower. Depart Sotogrande around 10:30, cruise 18 NM east with the morning sun behind you, and drop anchor at Río Verde — the famous swim cove on the Golden Mile — for a long final lunch and the last swim of the trip. Many guests use the SeaBob, paddleboards and giant flamingo inflatables here. We arrive back in Puerto Banús by 17:00 in time for disembarkation, photos and your transfer to Málaga airport, which is 45 minutes by road. If your forecast shows a strong Levante wind, the skipper can flip the route east-first and end with Sotogrande on day three instead. Which yachts suit a multi-day charter Multi-day means proper cabins, two heads, climate control and a galley. From our fleet, four yachts qualify: the Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 guests, 4 cabins), the Canados 86 (26m, 12 guests, 4 cabins, two crew), the Ferretti 94 (29m, 12 guests, 4 cabins plus separate crew quarters) and the Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m, 12 guests, 3 cabins for smaller groups). Day-boats like the Astondoa 40, Bandido or Speedboat do not have sleeping arrangements and are not suitable beyond an overnight at anchor . Pick the Ferretti 94 if you want full crew service throughout. Pricing, inclusions and what is extra Multi-day pricing is on request — message us on WhatsApp with dates and group size. Every quote includes the licensed skipper, fuel for the full 60–90 NM route, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, safety equipment and Spanish IVA at 21%. What is not included: dinners ashore, marina berths in Estepona or Sotogrande on the overnight nights, hotel rooms if you choose to sleep on land, and watersports fuel for jet ski or SeaBob beyond a courtesy session. Chef-prepared lunches and dinners on board can be added at €60–120 per head per meal — ask when you message us. Weather window and what to pack The cruising season runs May through October. June and September are the sweet spot: 22–28°C air, 20–23°C water, light Poniente breezes and a marina that is busy without being chaotic. July and August are warmer but the Levante wind can push swell into anchorages, so the skipper may reroute east-first. Pack soft-sided bags (rigid suitcases do not fit cabin lockers), reef-safe sunscreen, a long-sleeved layer for the sunset cruise back, swimwear for three days, one smarter outfit for the Sotogrande or Puerto Banús dinner, and chargers with EU plugs. Towels, snorkel masks and snorkels are already on board. How to book your multi-day charter Tell us your dates, group size and preferred yacht via WhatsApp. We hold the boat for 48 hours with no deposit while you confirm flights. A 50% deposit secures the dates, with the balance paid the morning of boarding. We can also build a 2-day, 4-day or 5-day variant — Gibraltar and Tarifa are within range with an early start. See our overnight yacht charter page for the short version of the same idea. Frequently asked questions Can we really sleep on board for two nights? Yes — the four yachts we use for multi-day trips all have proper cabins with double beds, air conditioning and en-suite heads. The Ferretti 94 has four guest cabins plus separate crew quarters, the Canados 86 and Mangusta 80 each have four cabins, and the Azimut 58 has three. You can also mix nights: one aboard, one in a marina hotel. How much does a 3-day yacht charter from Marbella cost? Pricing is on request because route, marinas and catering vary by group. As a rough guide, expect roughly four times a Tier B day rate of €4,719, plus marina berths (€80–250 per night in Sotogrande or Estepona), plus optional chef catering at €60–120 per head per meal. WhatsApp us with dates and group size for a firm quote. Which boats in your fleet suit multi-day cruising? The Ferretti 94, Canados 86 and Mangusta 80 are the three flagships built for overnight cruising — proper cabins, two heads minimum, full galley and stabilisers that keep things calm at anchor. The Azimut 58 Flybridge works for smaller groups of six to eight. Day-boats like the Bandido, Astondoa 40 and Mariah SX21 do not have sleeping cabins. Can we cross to Gibraltar or Morocco on a 3-day charter? Gibraltar yes — it sits 35 NM from Puerto Banús and is reachable on a 2-day or 3-day itinerary with an early start. Morocco crossings need customs paperwork, 48-hour notice for clearance and a different insurance class on most yachts. We can arrange a Tangier or Ceuta day-visit with two weeks of notice, but it is not part of the standard 3-day route. What are the best months for a multi-day charter? May, June, September and early October. Water sits at 19–23°C, the easterly Levante wind is less frequent and Puerto Banús is busy but not chaotic. July and August also work, but expect 28–32°C air, full marinas and possible reroutes. We do not recommend November through April — water drops to 16°C and weather windows shrink. Do we have to sleep at the same marina each night? No — that is the point of multi-day. Night one is usually Puerto Banús and night two is Sotogrande or Estepona, which gives you a different town for dinner and a real sense of progress. TITLE: New Year&#x27;s Eve on a Yacht in Marbella

Home › New Year's Eve on a Yacht in Marbella New Year's Eve on a yacht in Marbella means watching the Puerto Banús fireworks from the water with a glass of cava in hand, skipper at the helm, and your group spread across the deck instead of squeezed into a beach-club queue. Charters start at €749 for two hours on our Tier A boats — skipper, drinks and 21% IVA included — and scale up to the 24-metre Mangusta 80 for groups of twelve. This page covers what NYE looks like on board, which boat suits your party, what it costs, and how to lock in the date before mid-December when the diary closes. Why spend NYE on the water in Marbella The Costa del Sol is one of the few places in Europe where 31 December can still be spent comfortably outdoors. Air sits between 13°C and 17°C through the afternoon, the sea holds at 15-16°C, and the Sierra Blanca shelters the coast from northerly wind — so most evenings are flat or gently rippled. Add the Puerto Banús municipal fireworks at midnight, private displays along the Golden Mile and the lights of Marbella town behind La Concha, and you have a 360° view no land venue can match. Boat Rental Marbella runs all NYE charters from Puerto Banús with our own fleet — no third-party operators. The fireworks viewing line The official municipal display launches from the Puerto Banús breakwater shortly after the midnight bells. Your skipper anchors 200-400 metres offshore opposite the marina entrance, which puts the boat directly under the burst pattern and clear of the harbour's no-go zone. From the same anchor you also see private fireworks from beach clubs in Río Verde and villas above the Golden Mile, and the marina itself stays lit until around 03:00. Most skippers cut engines for 20 minutes either side of midnight so the deck stays silent for the toast. Pricing for NYE charters NYE itself does not carry a surcharge on our fleet — you pay the published rate, with extra cava added free for the midnight toast. Full NYE (20:00-02:00, 6h on Mangusta 80 or Ferretti 94) — DJ on deck, catered tasting menu, midnight at anchor, slow cruise back along the Golden Mile lights. What to wear and bring Temperatures drop noticeably once you leave the breakwater. Plan for 10-12°C at midnight with a light 5-10 kt breeze. Bring a windproof jacket, a warm layer, a hat and closed shoes with non-marking soles. Heels are fine inside the saloon but awkward on a wet deck. Phones and cameras work well from the flybridge; if you want long-exposure fireworks shots, a small tripod fits any of our boats. Glassware and crockery are provided — no need to bring anything edible or alcoholic, though you are welcome to. Weather, safety and contingency We track AEMET forecasts daily from 24 December onwards. If wind exceeds 15 knots or swell tops 1.5 m, the skipper shortens the offshore leg and anchors closer to the harbour wall — the fireworks view is unaffected. In the rare case of a full small-craft warning, you can rebook to any night in January or take a 100% credit. All boats carry full lifejacket sets, EPIRB and twin VHF, and skippers hold a Spanish PER licence or higher. TITLE: Overnight on a Yacht in Marbella: Stay Aboard at Puerto Banús
DESCRIPTION: Overnight yacht Marbella: stay aboard at Puerto Banús from €2,299/8h. Skipper, drinks and breakfast options. Book your private cabin charter on WhatsApp.

Home › Overnight on a Yacht in Marbella: Stay Aboard at Puerto Banús An overnight yacht stay in Marbella means sleeping aboard a private charter berthed in Puerto Banús, not anchored offshore. Spanish maritime regulations do not allow overnight anchoring inside the coastal protection zone here, so every reputable operator (us included) runs overnights as stay-aboard at the slip with shore power, A/C and security gate access. From our Marbella fleet , prices start at €2,299 for a Tier A day package and extend into the night for a flat add-on quoted on WhatsApp. What "overnight" actually means in Marbella You charter the boat for a day (typically 8 hours of cruising) and then keep exclusive use of it through the night while it stays in its berth at Puerto Banús. The skipper finishes their shift around 19:00-20:00, hands you the keys to the cabins and saloon, and you have the boat to yourselves until breakfast. No engines, no anchoring, no swell — just a quiet floating apartment 90 seconds from Puerto Banús nightlife . This is different from what some Mediterranean ports allow (overnight anchorages in protected bays). Marbella's Posidonia seagrass protection zone, enforced by the Junta de Andalucía, prohibits overnight anchoring within roughly 2 nautical miles of the coastline. The stay-aboard berth format keeps you legal and considerably more comfortable. All of that is in the headline price. A realistic overnight itinerary The pattern most guests follow looks like this. Boarding at 11:00 in Puerto Banús . Cruise west along the Golden Mile, passing La Concha mountain on your right, then anchor at Río Verde for a two-hour swim stop in 4-6m of clear water. Lunch aboard while the skipper repositions to Cala del Faro near Marbella lighthouse. Afternoon swim, second cocktail. Slow return cruise at 18:30 timed for the sunset behind Gibraltar on a clear day. Back in the slip by 20:00, the skipper finishes their handover and you walk five minutes into Puerto Banús for dinner — or stay aboard for a chef-prepared meal on the flybridge. Lights out whenever; the marina security gate is staffed 24/7. Breakfast on board around 09:00, and either disembark or extend into a second day cruise. Sleeping comfort: what to expect Puerto Banús harbour has effectively zero swell because of its breakwater design. The most you will feel is the gentle slap of water against the hull and occasional wash from the harbour patrol. Cabins on every boat from the Azimut 39 upward have proper mattresses (not cushions), bedding, blackout curtains and reading lights. A/C runs off shore power all night without ticking down the boat's batteries. July and August nights in Marbella sit around 22-24°C with low humidity, so A/C is essential. Average sea temperature in July is 22°C, climbing to 23°C in August, which makes morning swims off the swim platform genuinely pleasant. We provide one full set of towels per guest; bring your own if you want a second set for the morning swim. Sunday-to-Monday overnights are quieter in the harbour than Friday/Saturday because the day-tripper traffic drops. Best time of day to sail with kids The Costa del Sol coast follows a predictable wind pattern: glassy at dawn, light easterly by 11:00, building westerly by 14:00, easing again after 18:00. For children under 6, the 09:00-12:00 morning slot is the calmest — flat water, mild sun, fewer crowds at the anchorage. Older kids and teenagers do well with the 17:00-19:00 sunset slot , which is cooler and offers the best photos. Avoid 13:00-15:00 in July and August when the surface chop and UV index both peak. Home › Experiences › Proposal on a Yacht The most-asked question we get from couples: "Can you help me propose on the boat?" Yes. We've done it a hundred times — and the skipper-coordinated script below is the playbook that actually works (vs the panicked-bag-of-Ring-Boxes approach). 2-hour sunset cruise, anchor stop at Río Verde, cava ready, ring hidden until the moment. €749 + a few small extras. How the skipper-coordinated proposal works You enquire by WhatsApp, we set up a quick voice call (skipper-to-proposer, 5 min) to coordinate: What does the partner know? Surprise sunset cruise, or surprise proposal-on-the-cruise? Affects how the day unfolds. The ring. Bring it onboard hidden however you want — we'll secure it in the galley fridge / saloon drawer / skipper-pocket. Don't have it visible in carry-on at boarding. The moment. Default: anchor at Río Verde with La Concha behind, just before the sun touches the horizon. Cava poured. You step to the bow. We discreetly disappear. The signal. You tell us the signal before — usually "when I move to the bow, bring the cava and step back". The skipper has the discretion of someone who has done this a hundred times. The aftermath. Champagne pop. Photo set the skipper takes from the helm (your phone, or your photographer's phone if you've hired one). Cruise back to Puerto Banús with the music on. The 2-hour proposal cruise — hour by hour T-75 min before sunset — Boarding at Puerto Banús. Welcome drink at the dock (chilled cava). Skipper briefs you both on safety, you brief skipper privately on the signal. T-60 min — Cast off . Slow cruise west past Marbella Club and Puente Romano. The Golden Mile mansions are the warm-up. T-30 min — Anchor at Río Verde . Calm water. La Concha behind. Music down low. Skipper hands you both a glass. T-15 min — You step to the bow. Or wait for the signal you'd agreed. Skipper gets out of the frame. T-0 — The moment. Cava pop. The skipper takes the photo from the helm (with your camera or partner's). T+15 min — Cruise return at displacement speed. Sunset light, music on, you've got 30 minutes to call your families. T+45 min — Back at Puerto Banús. Photo on the dock. Dinner reservation we held for you at Casanis or La Sala. Restaurant booking ashore — we hold a 2-top reservation at Casanis, La Sala, Sea Grill or Skina (your choice). Confirms after the yes. Acoustic musician on board — Spanish guitar at sunset. €400. The classic Río Verde sunset shot The proposal photo every couple wants is: you (proposing) on the bow at the centre, partner looking back at you, La Concha mountain behind, sun touching the horizon to camera-right, water reflecting orange. The skipper knows the angle. We anchor with the boat oriented for it. If you've hired a photographer, they get the wide and the close, plus the candid afterwards. If not, the skipper takes a few from the helm — they're not Annie Leibovitz but they're solid. Best months for a proposal May, June, September are the sweet spot — sunset 20:30–21:00, water 19-22 °C, levante winds gentler than peak summer. July–August works but sunset is later (21:30+) and the Río Verde anchorage is busier. October sunsets are early (19:45) but the light is the warmest. Winter proposals happen but it's cold for outdoor cava. What we don't do Pre-printed signs that ruin the spontaneity (unless you specifically request). Drones flying over the boat at the moment (Spanish law restricts this; also kills the privacy). Fake "engine breakdown" so you have to step on deck (it's been pitched; we don't). Tell the partner anything before — we hold every secret. Pricing Base: €749 for the 2-hour sunset cruise. Add photographer (€200), custom flowers (€80), restaurant booking (free coordination, you pay at the restaurant). Most proposal couples spend around €900-€1,200 all-in including dinner ashore. Home › Romantic Dinner on a Yacht in Marbella: Catered at Anchor A romantic dinner on a yacht in Marbella means leaving Puerto Banús at golden hour, anchoring in a calm bay off the Golden Mile, and eating a catered three-course menu on deck while the coastline lights up behind you. Pricing starts at €749 for a 2h sunset slot on the Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39 — skipper, cava, white wine, beer, soft drinks and snacks already included — and scales to €4,719 for 4h on the Mangusta 80 if you want a flybridge to yourselves. What you actually get for the price Every charter on our fleet includes the same baseline: a licensed Spanish skipper, fuel, insurance, safety kit, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks (olives, crisps, fresh fruit) and the 21% IVA. That means the €749 ticket for a Tier A 2h sunset on the Astondoa 40 is the all-in price — there is no fuel surcharge or skipper tip waiting at the end of the night. Hot food is the only paid add-on, quoted per head on WhatsApp once you tell us the menu. Tier A (Astondoa 40, Azimut 39): €749/2h sunset or €2,299/8h full evening. The saloon below has a proper dining table and air-con if the wind picks up. For a milestone (engagement, anniversary, honeymoon night), the Mangusta 80 is the right call: 24m, master cabin, two sundecks, jet ski included. Boat Length Best for From Dubhe 8m Just the two of you, budget On request Astondoa 40 12.5m Standard romantic dinner €749/2h Azimut 39 12.5m Standard romantic dinner €749/2h Pershing 46 14m Faster cruise to Estepona On request Mangusta 80 24m Proposal, milestone, privacy €4,719/4h Where the yacht actually anchors We do not eat under way. The skipper cruises 10-25 minutes from Puerto Banús, drops anchor in a sheltered bay, kills the engines, and dinner begins. The three regular anchorages are Río Verde just off the Golden Mile (closest, best Marbella skyline view), Cala del Faro near the Marbella lighthouse (quietest, deepest water), and Cala Cortés towards Estepona (best for sunset over the western coast). The choice depends on wind direction on the night — typical evening conditions are 8-15 kts and 0.3-0.6m swell, which is flat enough for plates and stemmed glasses to behave. The food: what's free, what costs extra Drinks and light snacks are included on every charter, no exceptions. That means cava on ice when you board, plus white wine, beer, soft drinks, still and sparkling water, olives, crisps and fresh fruit through the night. Hot catered courses are arranged separately. We work with a local caterer in Puerto Banús who delivers sealed insulated boxes to the boat 30 minutes before departure. Standard menus run €45-90 per head and cover paella for two, grilled dorada or sea bass, tuna tataki, vegetarian (grilled vegetable platter, burrata salad), and a dessert plate. Vegan, gluten-free and halal are all available with 48h notice. Champagne upgrade (Veuve Clicquot, Moët, Dom Pérignon): charged at cost, no corkage. Wine pairing upgrade: €25-60 per person depending on the bottle. External catering: bring your own, no corkage, no fee. Timing: when to board for the perfect sunset Marbella sunsets fall between 21:00 and 22:00 from May to September. The trick is boarding 90 minutes before sunset so you have time to cruise out, anchor, pour the first glass and watch the light change. In July-August we usually board at 19:30 and return by 21:30 for a 2h sunset slot, or 18:00-02:00 for the 8h package which lets you stay out under the stars. April and October still work — sea temperature is 16-18°C and evenings are mostly calm — but you'll want a layer once the sun is down. The wind reliably drops after sundown along this coast, which is why dinner at anchor here is more stable than in many Mediterranean ports. Setting the scene: flowers, music, proposals If you message us on WhatsApp at least 48h ahead, we can stage the boat before you arrive. That usually means white roses on the saloon table, a chilled bottle of your preferred champagne, a Bluetooth speaker pre-paired with your phone and pre-loaded with your playlist, and the skipper briefed on the exact anchor spot you want for the proposal moment. We do not film or photograph guests without consent — if you want a photographer on board, bring your own, and we'll keep the skipper out of frame. Safety, stability and the weather call The bays west of Puerto Banús are sheltered by the coastline and the Sierra Blanca mountain range, which is the main reason evening swell is so low. DESCRIPTION: Half-day sailing experience Marbella aboard the Lagoon 380 catamaran: 4 hours, up to 10 guests, drinks + skipper included. Book by WhatsApp today.

Home › Sailing Experience Marbella: Half-Day on the Lagoon 380 A half-day sailing experience in Marbella on our Lagoon 380 catamaran runs four hours from Puerto Banús, costs are confirmed on request via WhatsApp, and includes a licensed skipper, drinks, snacks and 21% IVA. You sail roughly 12-18 nautical miles between Río Verde and Cala del Faro, with two swim stops and zero engine noise once the sails are up. The Lagoon 380 takes up to 10 guests across two hulls, which means almost no heel — even non-sailors and small children settle in within minutes. Start your planning from our main fleet hub or jump straight to the Lagoon 380 page for specs. What the half-day actually looks like You arrive at Puerto Banús 15 minutes before departure and meet your skipper at the catamaran pontoon. After a five-minute safety brief, we motor out of the harbour and hoist the main and genoa once we clear the breakwater — usually around the 1 NM mark west of the entrance. From there it is sailing, not motoring: on a typical July afternoon with 14 kts of Poniente, the Lagoon 380 makes 6-7 knots on a beam reach without straining anything. The first anchor stop is off Río Verde, roughly 4 NM west of Puerto Banús, where the water is calm in westerly wind and the bottom is sand at 8-10 metres. Guests swim, snorkel or use the swim ladder for 30-40 minutes while we lay out snacks in the cockpit. Then we either continue west toward Cala del Faro near Cabopino for a second stop, or tack back along the Golden Mile if the wind has clocked. The half-day ends back at Puerto Banús by 14:00 or 19:00 depending on slot. Why a catamaran beats a monohull for this trip The Lagoon 380 has two hulls 6.5 metres apart, which gives it three advantages for a Marbella day-sail. First, it barely heels — guests can walk, eat and photograph from anywhere on deck without bracing. Second, the forward trampoline doubles the usable lounging area compared to a same-length monohull. Third, the shallow 1.15m draft lets us anchor closer to the beach in Cala del Faro and Cala Cortés than a 12m monohull could. The trade-off is upwind performance: a Lagoon 380 sails about 50° to the true wind versus 35-40° on a racing monohull. In practice this only matters when the Levante (easterly) is blowing hard against our route home, which is rare in summer. Compare with our Azimut 39 motor yacht if you prefer pure speed, or browse the full fleet for other options. Wind, weather and the Marbella sailing season Marbella's prevailing breeze is the Poniente, a westerly that fills in around 11:00 and peaks at 12-18 knots by mid-afternoon between May and October. This is the wind we plan around. The Lagoon 380 sails comfortably from 6 kts upward and we reef the main at 18 kts to keep things relaxed. Above 25 kts of Levante (easterly, gusty and choppy) we do not sail — those days get rescheduled at our cost. TITLE: Seabob Experience Marbella: Underwater Scooter Rental
DESCRIPTION: Seabob experience Marbella: ride the silent F5/F5 S underwater scooter from Puerto Banús, 4 speed levels, 40 min sessions. Book on WhatsApp now.

Home › Seabob Experience Marbella: Underwater Scooter Rental A Seabob experience in Marbella turns an ordinary boat day into something genuinely different: instead of just swimming off the back of the boat, you grip a 29 kg underwater scooter, squeeze the trigger, and pull yourself through the water at up to 20 km/h. We carry the Cayago Seabob F5/F5 S on board our Puerto Banús fleet , so it slots into any half-day or full-day charter. This guide covers how it actually works, where we use it, what it costs, and which of our boats pair with it best. The hub for all our charters and add-ons lives at Boat Rental Marbella . What the Seabob is — and why people book one The Seabob F5 is a German-built jet-propulsion craft about 1.5 m long, weighing 29 kg in air and almost neutrally buoyant in water. Four power levels run from a gentle 6 km/h up to a punchy 20 km/h on the surface, and it dives to a maximum depth of 6 m. There is no exhaust, no fuel smell, no engine noise — just a quiet electric whirr and the rush of water past your mask. That silence is the reason guests keep extending sessions: you can drift up on a shoal of bream or a curious octopus without spooking it. Where we launch it along the Marbella coast We don't take the Seabob out in busy swim zones or near beach buoy lines. The skipper picks an anchorage with clear water, sandy bottom and limited boat traffic. The standard rotation: Cala del Faro — 10 minutes east of Puerto Banús, 4-8 m depth, sandy bottom with rock fingers. Our default. Río Verde — 5 minutes west, calm on Levante days, good for first-timers. Cala Cortés near Cabopino — 25 minutes east, clearer visibility, worth the run on a full-day. South-east of Marbella Marina — sheltered when the Poniente blows. For a deeper look at where each spot sits along the coast, see our best anchorages guide. How a Seabob session actually runs You board the boat at Puerto Banús, the skipper runs through the standard safety brief, and we cruise to the chosen anchorage. Once anchored, the crew lifts the Seabob off its rack and lowers it to the bathing platform. The rider-side briefing takes 5-10 minutes: how to trim the device with body weight, when to use Power 1 versus Power 4, the kill-switch lanyard, and the 10 m exclusion zone around other swimmers. Then you're in the water. Most guests run a five-minute surface familiarisation, then start diving the device — 1 m, 2 m, then down to 5 or 6 m if their ears equalise comfortably. Battery life, rotation and group size One full battery gives roughly 60 minutes of mixed-depth riding. On a four-guest charter we typically run 15-20 minute sessions per rider, which is honestly plenty — the device pulls hard against your forearms and your grip tires faster than your lungs. For groups of six or more on a yacht like the Canados 86 or Ferretti 94 , we'll carry a spare battery so the rotation doesn't stall. Which of our boats pair best with the Seabob Any boat in our fleet can carry the Seabob, but the experience improves dramatically with a proper bathing platform and a freshwater rinse on deck. On a Seabob session we add mask, snorkel and fins for each rider, a buoyancy-controlled briefing, and a charged battery (plus a spare on longer trips). What's not included: GoPro footage (bring your own with a wrist tether), wetsuits outside peak summer, or a separate Seabob-only outing — we run it strictly as an add-on to a boat charter. Pricing and how to book Seabob pricing depends on the boat you pick and the session length, so we don't publish a flat rate. The boat charter itself follows our standard tiers: €749 for two hours on an Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39 , scaling to €2,299 for a full eight hours, and €4,719 minimum four hours on a Mangusta 80 (which already includes a free jet ski). The Seabob fee is added on top. Message us on WhatsApp with your date, group size and preferred boat — we'll come back with a fixed quote within an hour during office time. Safety, age limits and weather rules Minimum age for solo riding is 14, confident-swimmer standard. Under-16s wear a lifejacket in the water regardless of ability. The kill-switch lanyard clips to your wrist and cuts the motor the second you release it. We don't run Seabob sessions in swell over 0.7 m or wind above 18 kts — the device handles it, but launch and recovery off the swim platform become awkward and the visibility drops below useful. On those days the skipper will offer a sheltered alternative or move the run to the next morning. Frequently asked questions What exactly is a Seabob and how does it work in Marbella? A Seabob is a battery-powered underwater scooter built by German firm Cayago. Every charter includes a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, safety equipment, and Spanish VAT (21%). For Tier A boats, all-in pricing is €749 for 2 hours or €2,299 for 8 hours. Can I sketch from the boat while it's moving? Yes, but most artists prefer to anchor in calm coves like Cala del Faro or Cala Cortés. The skipper can find sheltered spots with stable water, making sketching easier. You can also sketch from the flybridge or bow while cruising at slow speeds. What landmarks can I sketch from the water? Popular subjects include La Concha mountain, the Golden Mile villas, Marbella's old town skyline, Puerto Banús' luxury yachts, and the rugged cliffs of Cala Cortés. Your skipper can suggest the best angles and anchor points. Do I need to bring my own art supplies? You're welcome to bring your own sketchbooks, pencils, or watercolours. We can also provide basic materials (paper, graphite pencils, watercolour sets) if you request them when booking. Just let us know via WhatsApp. How do I book a sketching charter? Book directly via WhatsApp. Mention you want a plein-air sketching experience, your preferred date and duration, and any special requests (e.g., art supplies, dietary snacks).
